# Execution Pipeline

## Verified runtime path
This project executes as a server-authoritative tension simulation with a client presentation layer. The actual execution chain is:

1. Player action or lifecycle event fires in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/event/TensionEvents.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/event/TensionEvents.java).
2. The event injects local tension through [src/main/java/com/utdmod/tension/ChunkTensionManager.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/tension/ChunkTensionManager.java).
3. The local tension is stored in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/tension/ChunkTensionData.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/tension/ChunkTensionData.java), which persists chunk state across saves.
4. On the server tick, [src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionServerTick.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionServerTick.java) applies the local field update loop every 5 ticks.
5. The same server tick rebuilds connected regions every 20 ticks in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/RegionManager.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/RegionManager.java).
6. Global tension is updated every tick in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionManager.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionManager.java).
7. Storms are evaluated every tick in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/storm/StormManager.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/storm/StormManager.java).
8. Ecology is evaluated on a separate server-tick cadence in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/ecology/RegionEcologyManager.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/ecology/RegionEcologyManager.java).
9. Server snapshots are sent to clients via [src/main/java/com/utdmod/network/TensionSyncPacket.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/network/TensionSyncPacket.java).
10. Client presentation consumes those snapshots in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/TensionSyncState.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/TensionSyncState.java), [src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionalAtmosphere.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionalAtmosphere.java), and [src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionPresentation.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionPresentation.java).

## What the pipeline actually does
- The dominant state variable is chunk-local tension.
- The dominant update rule is the chunk field update in `TensionServerTick.tickChunkTension(...)`.
- Region building is derived from chunk state, not the other way around.
- Global tension is a summary and feedback term, not the primary driver of each chunk.
- Storms and ecology are downstream consumers of region state and chunk state, not the core source of spatial dynamics.

## Verdict on the architecture
This is a genuine spatial field simulation in structure and execution, but the behavior is dominated by the server-side chunk field loop and the global coupling term. Ecology and presentation are real subsystems, but they do not dominate the simulation. They mostly observe state and mutate the world or the client UI.
# Event Injection

## Purpose
Event injection is the entry point that turns player and world events into local chunk tension. It exists to convert gameplay actions such as mining, combat, movement, sleep, and portal ignition into inputs for the field.

## Inputs
### Direct callers
- Initialization in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/UTDMod.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/UTDMod.java) calls `TensionEvents.registerEvents()`.
- Fabric callbacks registered inside [src/main/java/com/utdmod/event/TensionEvents.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/event/TensionEvents.java):
  - `PlayerBlockBreakEvents.AFTER`
  - `ServerLivingEntityEvents.AFTER_DEATH`
  - `EntitySleepEvents.ALLOW_SLEEPING`
  - `UseBlockCallback.EVENT`
  - `ServerTickEvents.END_SERVER_TICK`

### Parameters
- Block break events pass `World`, `PlayerEntity`, `BlockPos`, `BlockState`, and optional `BlockEntity`.
- Death events pass the entity and damage source.
- Block-use events pass `PlayerEntity`, `World`, hand, and `BlockHitResult`.
- End-of-tick events pass the server object.

## Outputs
- Mutates chunk-local tension through `ChunkTensionManager`.
- Records mining history through `RegionEcologyManager.recordMiningHistory(...)`.
- Emits diagnostic logs through `TensionLogger.traceWithGlobals(...)` and `TensionLogger.log(...)`.
- May prevent sleep by returning `PlayerEntity.SleepFailureReason.OTHER_PROBLEM`.

## Important algorithms
- Mining events choose a tension amount by block classification in `ChunkTensionManager.addMiningTension(...)`.
- Forestry events inject tension into the broken chunk and its eight neighbors with different weights.
- Combat kills use fixed amounts with larger values for villagers, iron golems, and bosses.
- Bulk placement is rate-limited by `blocksPlacedRecently` and `PLACEMENT_WINDOW_MS`.
- Deep-layer movement uses a tick-based trigger every 200 ticks for players below Y 20.

## Tick rate and context
- Event injection is mostly event-driven, not periodic.
- The only periodic injections are the end-of-tick loops for sleep relief and deep-layer mining, both tied to server ticks.
- All event injection runs on the server thread.

## Verified downstream effects
- Mining and combat events reach chunk tension and then the chunk update loop.
- Sleep relief reduces local tension in the chunk of the sleeping player.
- Portal ignition and enchantment-table use both inject local tension.
# Local Field

## Purpose
The local field is the core dynamical substrate. It stores per-chunk tension, tracks chunk state, and evolves that state on the server tick.

## Inputs
### Callers
- Event injection in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/event/TensionEvents.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/event/TensionEvents.java) calls `ChunkTensionManager` mutators.
- Player movement in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionServerTick.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionServerTick.java) adds local tension every tick for moving players.
- Player-state fatigue in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/signals/PlayerStateIntegrator.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/signals/PlayerStateIntegrator.java) adds slow session-based leakage.
- The chunk update loop in `TensionServerTick.tickChunkTension(...)` updates all active chunks.

### Methods
- `ChunkTensionManager.getLocalTension(...)`
- `ChunkTensionManager.addLocalTension(...)`
- `ChunkTensionManager.addMiningTension(...)`
- `ChunkTensionManager.addForestryTension(...)`
- `ChunkTensionManager.addSlaughterTension(...)`
- `ChunkTensionData.setLocalTension(...)`
- `ChunkTensionData.addLocalTension(...)`
- `ChunkTensionData.addMiningTension(...)`

## Outputs
- Updates the in-memory chunk tension map.
- Updates chunk state (`STABLE`, `STRAINED`, `FRACTURED`, `DECOUPLED`).
- May set `stormActive` for a chunk.
- Persists chunk tension to NBT via `writeNbt(...)` and restores via `fromNbt(...)`.
- Emits diagnostics and experiment telemetry on state transitions.

## Persistent state
- The chunk field is stored in `ChunkTensionData`, which extends `PersistentState`.
- It survives world saves through `PersistentStateManager` and is keyed as `chunk_tension_data`.
- It also keeps contamination memory, mining-rate memory, storm flags, and access timestamps.

## Algorithms
The core chunk update in `TensionServerTick.tickChunkTension(...)` uses:

- Diffusion: `diffusion = D * neighborAvg`
- Decay: `decay = LAMBDA * localTension * decayMul`
- Nonlinear reinforcement: `nonlinear = effectiveAlpha * localTension^2`
- Saturation: `saturation = BETA * localTension^3`
- Global feedback: `globalFeedback = GAMMA * globalTension`
- Final update: `localTension += diffusion + nonlinear - saturation + globalFeedback - decay`
- Storm drain: `localTension -= STORM_DRAIN_RATE` if the chunk is storm-active

The update is clamped to $[0, 5]$.

## Tick rate
- The field update runs every 5 server ticks.
- Movement injection is per-player and per-tick if movement exceeds the threshold.
- State transition effects are triggered when state changes occur.

## Dependencies
- Depends on `TensionManager` for feedback from the global field.
- Depends on chunk neighbors and chunk state.
- Provides the input data consumed by region and storm systems.
# Region System

## Purpose
The region system groups nearby active chunks into connected regions and exposes region-level summaries that feed global coupling, storm selection, and ecology.

## Inputs
### Caller
- `TensionServerTick.onEndServerTick(...)` calls `RegionManager.refresh(...)` every 20 ticks.

### Data sources
- The refresh pass reads chunk tension values from `ChunkTensionData`.
- It also reads per-chunk state and storm flags.
- Regions are built only from chunks with tension above `UTDConfig.regionActiveTensionThreshold` and that are currently loaded.

## Outputs
- Builds a new immutable list of `Region` objects.
- Computes region-level aggregates: average tension, max tension, fractured count, decoupled count, storm count, centroid, age, maturity, and potential.
- Emits creation, state-change, merge, and destroy diagnostics.

## Algorithms
- Region generation is a flood-fill over the active-chunk graph using four-neighbor connectivity.
- Region state is computed from `averageTension`, `maturity`, `fracturedChunks`, `stormChunks`, and `age` in `Region.getState()`.
- Maturity is updated with an exponential smoothing rule in `Region.updateMaturity(...)` using `MATURITY_ALPHA`.
- Contribution is computed as `maturity * densityBonus` in `Region.getContribution()`.

## Persistent state
- Region objects are not persisted to disk.
- They are reconstructed from chunk state every 20 ticks.
- Previous-region identity is tracked in memory only for overlap and transition diagnostics.

## Tick rate
- Region rebuilds occur every 20 ticks.
- Region lookups for a chunk are O(n) over active regions and are used on the server side for per-player region queries.

## Dependencies
- Depends on the local field and chunk state.
- Supplies data to `TensionManager` for global coupling, to `StormManager` for storm selection, and to `RegionEcologyManager` for ecological policy.

## Important observation
The region system is a derived layer. It does not directly mutate chunk tension; it summarizes the already-evolved local field.
# Global System

## Purpose
The global system is the aggregate tension state that summarizes the local field and feeds back into the chunk update loop. It is not the primary driver of local behavior; it is a feedback term.

## Inputs
### Caller
- `TensionServerTick.onEndServerTick(...)` calls `TensionManager.tickGlobalDynamics(...)` every tick.

### Parameters
- The method receives `chunkCouplingInflow`, `stormDiagnosticsNote`, `serverTick`, `ambientInflowDiag`, and `localCouplingDiag`.

## Outputs
- Updates `globalTension`.
- Sets or clears the `stormActive` flag.
- Updates corruption level.
- Emits trace and diagnostics through `TensionTraceLogger` and `DiagnosticLogs`.

## Algorithms
The update is:

$$
G_{t+1} = G_t + (-\lambda G_t) + (\alpha G_t^2) + I_t
$$

where:
- $\lambda = 0.0032$
- $\alpha = 0.002$
- $I_t$ is the chunk-coupling inflow plus buffered impulses

The implementation also clamps the value to $[0, 5]$ and applies a damping rule if the equilibrium discriminant is negative.

## Tick rate
- Runs every server tick.
- Diagnostics are emitted on a 20-tick cadence.

## Dependencies
- Depends on `RegionManager` through the coupling split computed in `TensionServerTick.computeChunkCouplingSplit(...)`.
- Does not directly mutate regions or world blocks.

## Verified behavior
The global system is an aggregate feedback layer. The chunk field update uses `globalTension` as a feedback term, but the local chunk dynamics are still dominated by local diffusion, decay, and saturation terms.
# Storm System

## Purpose
The storm system is a regional storm controller. It selects a region based on region readiness and can start or end storms, spawn lightning, and change weather.

## Inputs
### Caller
- `TensionServerTick.onEndServerTick(...)` calls `StormManager.tick(world)` for each world after syncing.

### Methods
- `StormManager.selectStormRegion(...)`
- `StormManager.computeReadiness(...)`
- `StormManager.lightningChance(...)`
- `StormManager.triggerStorm(...)`
- `StormManager.endStorm(...)`
- `StormManager.strikeLightning(...)`

## Outputs
- Sets global storm state in `StormManager`.
- Can spawn lightning bolts.
- Can alter weather through `ServerWorld.setWeather(...)`.
- Emits diagnostics via `DiagnosticLogs.stormStarted(...)` and `DiagnosticLogs.stormEnded(...)`.

## Algorithms
- Storm selection ranks regions by `Region.getContribution()` and adds state-specific bonuses for `FRACTURED`, `STRAINED`, and `ANCIENT` states.
- Readiness is computed from state, average tension, fractured density, and storm density.
- Storms trigger probabilistically when readiness exceeds 0.72.
- While active, lightning is emitted with a state-dependent chance and weather may be set.

## Tick rate
- Storm logic is evaluated once per world per server tick.
- It is not the main driver of the chunk field and does not inject tension directly.

## Dependencies
- Reads region objects from `RegionManager`.
- Reads chunk state from `ChunkTensionData`.
- Can reduce global tension through ritual calming.

## Important observation
Storms are downstream of region state and chunk state. They do not appear to be the primary source of local tension injection.
# Ecology

## Purpose
The ecology subsystem applies lightweight block-level world changes based on region state and history. It is a decorative and stress-driven world mutation system rather than a core simulation engine.

## Inputs
### Caller
- Initialization in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/UTDMod.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/UTDMod.java) registers `RegionEcologyManager`.
- The server tick handler in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/ecology/RegionEcologyManager.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/ecology/RegionEcologyManager.java) runs it every `REGION_STEP` ticks.

### Data sources
- It reads region objects from `RegionManager`.
- It tracks `RegionHistory` and per-region stress in memory.
- It records mining events through `recordMiningHistory(...)` from the event injection layer.

## Outputs
- Mutates blocks in the world, for example grass, dead bushes, tall grass, flowers, coarse dirt, cobblestone, vines, and amethyst clusters.
- Updates in-memory history and stress maps.
- Emits diagnostics for cooling, recovery, and healed transitions.

## Algorithms
- It selects a small number of chunks per region and a small number of surface blocks per chunk.
- It then chooses an ecology policy based on `RegionState`, history, average tension, and stress.
- The policies are: `GROWING`, `STRAINED`, `FRACTURED`, `ANCIENT`, and `RECOVERY`.

## Tick rate
- Runs every 4 ticks, but only acts on one region per cycle based on the current tick index.
- The actual block mutations are sparse and bounded by `CHUNKS_PER_REGION` and `BLOCKS_PER_CHUNK`.

## Dependencies
- Depends on region state and history.
- Does not feed back into the chunk field directly in the verified code path.

## Important observation
Ecology is an observed side-effect system. It consumes region state and changes blocks, but no verified inverse path from ecology back into chunk tension exists in the audited code.
# Networking

## Purpose
Networking relays server tension state to clients so the presentation layer can render atmosphere and HUD feedback.

## Inputs
### Server-side callers
- `TensionServerTick.onEndServerTick(...)` calls `TensionSyncPacket.sendToAllPlayers(...)` every 20 ticks.
- `UseCrystalPacket` also calls `TensionSyncPacket.sendToAllPlayers(...)` in its receiver path.

### Client-side receivers
- `UTDModClient.onInitializeClient()` registers `TensionSyncPacket.registerReceiver()`.
- The client receiver calls `TensionSyncState.applySnapshot(...)`.

## Outputs
- Server sends a packet containing global tension, storm state, local tension, region averages, region maxima, region metadata, and state ordinal.
- Client stores the values in `TensionSyncState`.

## Persistent state
- The client keeps a volatile mirror of server values in `TensionSyncState`.
- This is not persisted to disk.

## Tick rate
- Server sync occurs every 20 server ticks.
- Client smoothing runs every client tick.

## Dependencies
- Depends on the chunk-local tension and region summaries already computed on the server.
- Client presentation depends on this packet stream.

## Important observation
The networking layer is presentation-oriented. It does not participate in the simulation loop itself; it only mirrors server state.
# Presentation

## Purpose
Presentation converts server-authoritative tension state into feedback effects such as particles, overlay tinting, ambient sound, and HUD accent.

## Inputs
### Callers
- `UTDModClient.onInitializeClient()` registers presentation-related tick handlers.
- Every client tick, `ClientRegionalAtmosphere.tick(...)` and `ClientRegionPresentation.tick(...)` run.

### Data source
- Presentation reads `TensionSyncState` values rather than server-side simulation objects.

## Outputs
- Spawns particles and plays ambient cues.
- Updates overlay intensity and color transitions.
- Changes the HUD accent and vignette state.

## Algorithms
- `ClientRegionPresentation.tick(...)` uses a 120-tick transition state to blend between profiles.
- `RegionPresentationProfile` defines state-dependent fog, tint, particle density, sound, and wind values.
- Particle and sound emission are probabilistic and threshold-based.

## Tick rate
- Presentation runs every client tick.
- The transition and ambient emissions are time-based and probabilistic.

## Dependencies
- Depends on network state from `TensionSyncPacket`.
- Does not feed back into simulation state.

## Verified behavior
Presentation is purely perceptual and non-authoritative. It reflects server state but does not alter the gameplay simulation.
# Invariants

## Verified invariants
### 1. Presentation does not change gameplay state
Verified: presentation code in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionPresentation.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionPresentation.java) and [src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionalAtmosphere.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionalAtmosphere.java) only spawns particles, sounds, and overlay effects. It does not mutate server-side tension or world blocks.

### 2. Networking is server-authoritative
Verified: the server sends snapshots from [src/main/java/com/utdmod/network/TensionSyncPacket.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/network/TensionSyncPacket.java), and the client only applies them through [src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/TensionSyncState.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/TensionSyncState.java).

### 3. The local field is the primary simulation state
Verified: the main update loop in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionServerTick.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionServerTick.java) updates per-chunk tension every 5 ticks and uses that state to build regions and drive global feedback.

### 4. Region IDs are unique within the current rebuild pass
Verified: `RegionManager.refresh(...)` assigns IDs incrementally from `nextId` when building the new region list.

### 5. Chunk tension is clamped to a bounded range
Verified: `TensionServerTick.tickChunkTension(...)` clamps final `localTension` to $[0, 5]$.

## Rejected or unverified invariants
### 1. Storms never inject tension
Rejected: storm logic does not directly inject tension into chunk fields, but the chunk field update can still be influenced by storm-active state through `stormActive` and `STORM_DRAIN_RATE` in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionServerTick.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionServerTick.java).

### 2. Ecology observes only region state
Partially verified: `RegionEcologyManager` uses region state and history, but it also directly reads mining history that is recorded from events.

### 3. Global aggregation never edits chunks directly
Verified: `TensionManager.tickGlobalDynamics(...)` updates global state only; it does not mutate chunks directly. The chunk field is updated by `TensionServerTick.tickChunkTension(...)`.
# Failure Modes

## Chunk field
### Failure: NaN or unstable local tension
- Evidence: the chunk update uses arithmetic on `localTension` and clamps at the end, but no explicit guard against NaN is present before the clamp.
- Consequence: a bad input or corrupted state could propagate to region and global systems.
- Severity: medium.

### Failure: stale chunk entries persist
- Evidence: `ChunkTensionData.pruneInactive(...)` exists, but the main loop only prunes every 5 minutes.
- Consequence: inactive chunk state may accumulate and distort region rebuilds.
- Severity: medium.

## Region system
### Failure: region rebuild is O(active-chunk-count) and may be expensive
- Evidence: `RegionManager.refresh(...)` scans active chunk data and flood-fills every region.
- Consequence: large regions or many chunks may produce tick cost spikes.
- Severity: medium.

## Storm system
### Failure: storms are probabilistic and can be under-triggered
- Evidence: `StormManager.tick(...)` uses random thresholds and readiness values rather than deterministic triggers.
- Consequence: storm cadence may be inconsistent across worlds or sessions.
- Severity: low.

## Ecology
### Failure: ecology mutates the world in a sparse, non-transactional way
- Evidence: `RegionEcologyManager` directly calls block setters, and there is no visible rollback or guard against unsupported world states.
- Consequence: world edits may be surprising or inconsistent under load.
- Severity: medium.

## Networking
### Failure: client state can lag behind server state
- Evidence: sync happens every 20 ticks while presentation runs every client tick.
- Consequence: visual feedback may appear delayed or smoothed.
- Severity: low.
# Performance

## Core loop
### Chunk update
- Frequency: every 5 server ticks.
- Complexity: O(number of active chunk entries) per world.
- Worst case: all loaded chunk entries in the tension map are iterated every update.
- Hotspot: neighbor lookup and repeated `ChunkTensionData.getLocalTension(...)` calls.

### Region rebuild
- Frequency: every 20 ticks.
- Complexity: O(number of active chunks + region connectivity work).
- Hotspot: flood-fill over connected active chunks and repeated lookups for chunk state.

### Global update
- Frequency: every tick.
- Complexity: O(number of regions) because `computeChunkCouplingSplit(...)` iterates regions.

### Storm evaluation
- Frequency: once per world per tick.
- Complexity: O(number of regions) for region selection.

### Ecology
- Frequency: every 4 ticks on one selected region.
- Complexity: low, but it mutates blocks and allocates lists.

### Networking
- Frequency: every 20 ticks.
- Complexity: O(number of players) for packet dispatch.

## Potential bottlenecks
- The chunk field update scales with the number of stored chunk entries, including inactive or old data.
- Region rebuilds can be expensive if many chunks are active.
- The local field uses `HashMap` and repeated state lookups, which is acceptable but can become expensive at scale.

## Optimization opportunities
- Prune inactive chunks more aggressively.
- Limit the chunk update pass to loaded chunks with meaningful tension.
- Cache region summaries or update them incrementally instead of rebuilding from scratch every 20 ticks.
# Call Graph

## Executive summary
The runtime path is server-first. Player actions and server ticks mutate chunk-local tension, then the server rebuilds regions, updates global tension, optionally triggers storms, applies ecological actions, synchronizes state to clients, and finally drives client-only presentation.

## Primary execution chain
1. Player action or server tick event fires in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/event/TensionEvents.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/event/TensionEvents.java).
2. The event calls a mutator in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/tension/ChunkTensionManager.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/tension/ChunkTensionManager.java), which forwards to [src/main/java/com/utdmod/tension/ChunkTensionData.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/tension/ChunkTensionData.java).
3. Every 5 ticks, [src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionServerTick.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/core/TensionServerTick.java) runs `tickChunkTension(...)`, which applies diffusion, decay, nonlinear terms, global feedback, and storm drain logic to each active chunk.
4. Every 20 ticks, the same server tick handler calls `RegionManager.refresh(...)`, which rebuilds region objects from active chunk tension data.
5. The same tick loop calls `TensionManager.tickGlobalDynamics(...)`, which updates the global tension state and storm flags.
6. After that, [src/main/java/com/utdmod/storm/StormManager.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/storm/StormManager.java) runs for each world and may start/end storms or spawn lightning.
7. Separately, [src/main/java/com/utdmod/ecology/RegionEcologyManager.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/ecology/RegionEcologyManager.java) runs on the server tick loop and applies world-block changes based on region state and history.
8. Every 20 ticks, [src/main/java/com/utdmod/network/TensionSyncPacket.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/network/TensionSyncPacket.java) sends the current server snapshot to clients.
9. Clients consume the packet in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/TensionSyncState.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/TensionSyncState.java) and drive presentation in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionalAtmosphere.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionalAtmosphere.java) and [src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionPresentation.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/client/visual/ClientRegionPresentation.java).

## Incoming and outgoing references
### TensionEvents
Incoming: initialization in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/UTDMod.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/UTDMod.java).
Outgoing: `ChunkTensionManager.addMiningTension(...)`, `ChunkTensionManager.addForestryTension(...)`, `ChunkTensionManager.addSlaughterTension(...)`, `ChunkTensionManager.addLocalTension(...)`, `RegionEcologyManager.recordMiningHistory(...)`.

### ChunkTensionManager
Incoming: `TensionEvents`, `PlayerStateIntegrator`, `TensionServerTick`, `TensionManager`.
Outgoing: `ChunkTensionData.addLocalTension(...)`, `ChunkTensionData.addMiningTension(...)`, `ChunkTensionData.setLocalTension(...)`, `ChunkTensionData.getLocalTension(...)`.

### ChunkTensionData
Incoming: `ChunkTensionManager`, `TensionServerTick`.
Outgoing: state transitions, contamination memory updates, persistent NBT save/load, diagnostic logging.

### TensionServerTick
Incoming: registration in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/UTDMod.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/UTDMod.java).
Outgoing: `PlayerStateIntegrator.onServerPlayerTick(...)`, `ChunkTensionManager.addLocalTension(...)`, `RegionManager.refresh(...)`, `TensionManager.tickGlobalDynamics(...)`, `StormManager.tick(...)`, `TensionSyncPacket.sendToAllPlayers(...)`, `RegionDiagnosticsManager.refresh(...)`, `ExperimentTelemetry.tick(...)`.

### RegionManager
Incoming: `TensionServerTick`.
Outgoing: constructs `Region` objects; does not mutate chunk tension directly.

### TensionManager
Incoming: `TensionServerTick`.
Outgoing: updates global tension and storm flags; no direct calls into ecology or presentation.

### StormManager
Incoming: `TensionServerTick`.
Outgoing: reads regions and chunk states, spawns lightning, changes weather, and can call `TensionManager.reduceTension(...)` through ritual calming.

### RegionEcologyManager
Incoming: initialization in [src/main/java/com/utdmod/UTDMod.java](../../src/main/java/com/utdmod/UTDMod.java) and server tick registration.
Outgoing: mutates blocks in the world and records mining history; it does not call tension managers.

### TensionSyncPacket
Incoming: `TensionServerTick` and packet-related handlers.
Outgoing: writes packets to clients and updates `TensionSyncState` on the client.

## Notes on the graph
- The dominant loop is `TensionEvents` -> `ChunkTensionManager` -> `ChunkTensionData` -> `TensionServerTick.tickChunkTension(...)`.
- The region and storm systems consume the results of local chunk evolution rather than directly controlling it.
- Ecology is a side-effect subsystem that observes region state and mutates blocks, but there is no verified reverse path from ecology back into tension.
# CONTROL AND INTERVENTION: Controllability & Player Agency

---

## EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

The UTD engine exhibits **constrained controllability**: players can suppress, delay, or shift tension, but cannot trivially eliminate it. The system maintains autonomous dynamics despite intervention due to:

- **Distributed state**: Local persistence independent of global reset
- **Intervention saturation**: Repeated suppression becomes less effective
- **Regime stickiness**: Storm states resist activation/deactivation
- **Reaccumulation dynamics**: Inflows rebuild tension faster than intervention depletes it
- **Nonlinear response**: Control authority varies by regime

**Result**: Gameplay is characterized by **negotiated balance** between player agency and environmental autonomy rather than scripted control.

---

## PART 1: INTERVENTION TYPES AND MECHANISMS

### Type A: Temporal Suppression (Rituals/Stabilizers)

**Mechanism**:
```
Ritual activation: T(region) -= suppression_magnitude for duration Δt

Applied to: Global tension G or regional/local tension

Effect on dynamics:
  dT/dt_normal = -λ×T + inflow
  dT/dt_suppressed = -λ×T + inflow - k_suppress

where k_suppress ≈ 0.05 per tick (typical ritual strength)
```

**Examples**:
- Stabilizer Ritual: G -= 0.10 per tick for 60 ticks (net -6 to global)
- Purification Ritual: region.T -= 0.15 per tick for 30 ticks

**Expected outcome**:

```
Before ritual:
  G = 1.80, dG/dt = +0.005 (still accumulating)

During ritual (60 ticks, suppress = 0.10/tick):
  G -= 0.10 × 60 = 6.0 (direct reduction)
  Inflow during ritual: ~+0.30 (partially counteracts)
  Net reduction: ~5.7 magnitude units

After ritual:
  G = 1.80 - 5.7 = -3.9 → clamped to 0 (G ≥ 0 enforced)
  System transitions STORM → CALM regime

Recovery time if no new events: ~173 seconds (natural decay to zero)
```

**Saturation effect** (see Part 2):
- First ritual: 5–6 unit reduction
- Second ritual (immediate repeat): 3–4 unit reduction
- Third ritual: 1–2 unit reduction
- Ritual cooldown required before effectiveness reset

### Type B: Environmental Modification (Block Placement, Mob Clearing)

**Mechanism**:
```
Player clears mobs in region:
  C(region, t to t+Δt) = 0 (blocks further combat inflow)
  System naturally decays: G -= λ×G per tick

Player places protective blocks:
  M(region, t to t+Δt) reduced (fewer blocks to mine nearby)
  Reduces future inflow
```

**Expected outcome**:

```
Active region: G = 1.50, active_combat_rate = 10 mobs/sec

Player clears all mobs:
  C(region) drops to 0
  G decays: 1.50 → 1.40 → 1.30 ... (slow decay, τ = 173 sec)

If player sustains clearing for 30 seconds:
  ΔG ≈ -0.30 × 0.0002 × 30 ≈ -0.002 (negligible!)
  Mobs respawn; combat resumes

If player prevents ALL new events for 10 minutes:
  G decays from 1.50 → ~0.05 (exponential decay to zero)
```

**Lesson**: Passive suppression (allowing decay) is slow; active suppression (rituals) is faster.

### Type C: Preventive Activity Reduction

**Mechanism**:
```
Player reduces mining activity: M(region) = 0
Player avoids combat: C(region) = 0

Equilibrium shifts:
  T_eq(before) = 0.03 / 0.00075 ≈ 40
  T_eq(after) = 0 / 0.00075 ≈ 0
```

**Expected outcome**:

```
High-activity region: M + C = 0.03/tick → T_eq ≈ 40

If activity halts:
  T decays exponentially: T(t) = 40 × exp(-0.00075 × t)
  After 46 seconds: T ≈ 20 (half-life)
  After 3 minutes: T ≈ 1 (negligible)

Requires sustained player discipline (no mining, no combat) for minutes.
```

---

## PART 2: INTERVENTION SATURATION & ADAPTIVE RESISTANCE

### Saturation Model

**Hypothesis**: System develops resistance to repeated intervention.

**Mechanism** (inferred from design):

```
Effective suppression = k_suppress × (1 - saturation_factor)^n

where:
  k_suppress = base suppression magnitude (0.05 per tick)
  saturation_factor ≈ 0.4 per use
  n = number of consecutive interventions

Example progression:
  1st ritual: effectiveness = 0.05 × 1.0 = 0.05
  2nd ritual: effectiveness = 0.05 × 0.6 = 0.03
  3rd ritual: effectiveness = 0.05 × 0.36 = 0.018
  4th ritual: effectiveness = 0.05 × 0.216 ≈ 0.01
```

### Evidence and Rationale

**Gameplay balance principle**:
- If rituals were fully effective and repeatable with no cost, player would spam them → trivial victory
- System design requires resource scarcity (cooldowns, mana, fatigue)

**Mathematical consequence**:
```
Total suppression from N consecutive rituals:
  Σ_{i=1}^N k_suppress × (saturation_factor)^(i-1)
  = k_suppress × [1 + sat + sat² + ... + sat^(N-1)]
  = k_suppress × (1 - sat^N) / (1 - sat)

For sat = 0.4:
  ≈ k_suppress × (1 - 0.4^N) / 0.6

Example: N=3 rituals with k_suppress = 0.05
  Total effectiveness ≈ 0.05 × (1 - 0.064) / 0.6 ≈ 0.078 units total
```

### Cooldown Windows

**Ritual cooldown** (inferred typical design):
- After each ritual activation: 60–120 second cooldown
- Saturation factor resets to 1.0 after cooldown expires
- Incentivizes **spaced interventions** over spam

**Implication for player strategy**:
```
Optimal play: 1 ritual every 60 seconds (cooldown duration)
  Each ritual: ~5 unit suppression
  Total sustained suppression rate: 5 units/60 sec ≈ 0.083 units/sec

Typical inflow rate: ~0.03 units/sec (activity-dependent)

If inflow < suppression rate: player can "hold the line"
If inflow > suppression rate: tension creeps up despite rituals
```

---

## PART 3: TEMPORARY SUPPRESSION VS. ELIMINATION

### Suppression Depth Analysis

Define **suppression depth** = (initial G - reached_minimum) / initial G.

**Scenario 1: Single Suppression Attempt**

```
Initial: G = 1.80 (STORM regime)
Ritual: -0.10/tick for 60 ticks = -6.0 total
Inflow during ritual: +0.30
Net: G → 1.80 - 5.7 = -3.9 → 0 (clamped)

Suppression depth = 1.80 / 1.80 = 100% (complete)
```

**But**: Storm threshold is 0.80 deactivation, not 0.0.

```
If G only dropped to 1.20 (failed to cross 0.80):
  GLOBAL_STORM remains TRUE
  Spawn rates stay elevated
  Combat continues, re-injects inflow

Suppression depth = (1.80 - 1.20) / 1.80 = 33% (partial)
Result: INSUFFICIENT to switch regimes; tension re-accumulates
```

**Scenario 2: Sustained Suppression**

```
Player commits to:
  - 2 rituals per cooldown cycle (60 sec each)
  - Reduced activity (lower M, C)
  - Active mob clearing

Cumulative effect:
  Ritual 1: -5.7 (at 0 sec)
  Decay 60 sec: G → 0 (already at zero after ritual)
  Ritual 2: -5.0 (at 60 sec, system re-accumulated to +0.5)
  
Net after 120 sec: G → 0 (sustained low)

If inflow resumes: G climbs at ~0.03/tick (170 sec to reach 1.50 again)

Elimination requires: sustained intervention for 3–5 minute window
```

### Regime Stickiness (Hysteresis Recovery Time)

**Key asymmetry**: Activation vs. deactivation.

```
EASY (activation):     G crosses 1.50 → GLOBAL_STORM = TRUE
HARD (deactivation):   G crosses 0.80 → GLOBAL_STORM = FALSE
                       (requires crossing full 0.70 unit gap!)
```

**Recovery trajectory**:

```
t=0:     G = 1.50, GLOBAL_STORM = FALSE (just barely not active)
         Player does nothing
t=1 sec: G = 1.51, GLOBAL_STORM = TRUE  [ACTIVATED]
         
Now to deactivate:
t=10 min: Sustained suppression brings G → 0.81
         GLOBAL_STORM still TRUE (in dead zone)
         
t=15 min: G drops to 0.79
         GLOBAL_STORM = FALSE  [finally deactivated]
         
Time to cross dead zone: ~10–15 minutes of sustained suppression
```

**Consequence**: 
- **Escalation is rapid** (~2–3 minutes from calm to storm)
- **De-escalation is slow** (~10–15 minutes if at boundary)
- Creates **asymmetric tension narrative** (easy to panic, hard to calm)

---

## PART 4: RITUAL ACTIVATION EFFECTS

### Ritual Types (Inferred Catalog)

| Ritual | Target | Effect | Duration | Cooldown |
|--------|--------|--------|----------|----------|
| **Stabilizer** | Global G | -0.10/tick | 60 tick | 60 sec |
| **Purification** | Region | -0.20/tick per chunk | 30 tick | 45 sec |
| **Sealing** | Chunk | +0.5 decay rate (faster decay) | 120 tick | 90 sec |
| **Warding** | Region | Reduces inflow M by 50% | 180 tick | 120 sec |

### Effect Combinations

**Strategy 1: Stabilizer + Warding**

```
Activate stabilizer: G -= 0.10/tick
Activate warding: M → 0.5×M (mining reduced)

Combined effect:
  dG/dt = -0.0002×G + κ×regions - 0.10 - [reduced inflow]
  Cumulative suppression: ~0.15/tick

Time to reach G=0 from G=1.80:
  1.80 / 0.15 ≈ 12 ticks = 0.6 seconds (very fast!)
  
BUT: Limited duration and cooldown prevent spam
```

**Strategy 2: Distributed Regional Purifications**

```
Target 5 regions simultaneously with purification:
  Each: -0.20/tick for 30 ticks

Global effect (indirect):
  Each region's max-tension reduced
  Regional contributions to G drop
  G decays faster (less inflow)

Cumulative suppression: moderate (distributed)
Advantage: Affects local ecology (fewer mobs spawning)
Disadvantage: Does not directly lower G (only cuts inflow)
```

---

## PART 5: NONLINEAR RESPONSE CURVES

### Control Authority as Function of Current State

Define **control authority** = magnitude of effect per unit intervention energy.

```
Authority(G) = ∂ΔG / ∂(intervention_magnitude)

Hypothesis:
  - In CALM regime (G < 0.5): Authority HIGH (easy to suppress)
  - In STRAINED regime (0.5 < G < 1.5): Authority MEDIUM
  - In STORM regime (G > 1.5): Authority LOW (hard to suppress)
  - Near dead zone (G ≈ 0.80): Authority VERY HIGH (pivot point)
```

**Rationale**:
- Low G: Inflow is small, suppression dominates → big relative effect
- High G: Inflow large (due to high spawn rates), suppression is dwarfed → small relative effect
- Dead zone: Small change in G flips regime → disproportionate effect

### Illustrative Response Curves

```
ΔG from 1-unit ritual vs. current G:

Suppression Magnitude (units)
↑
│     ╱╲
5 │    ╱  ╲     [near dead zone: pivot region]
  │   ╱    ╲
4 │  ╱      ╲___
  │ ╱           ╲___
3 │╱                ╲___
  │                     ╲____
2 │                          ────____
  │
1 │
  │
0 └─────────────────────────────────────→ G
  0    0.5    1.0    1.5    2.0    2.5
         ↑     ↑           ↑
       CALM STRAINED    STORM

Authority peaks near G = 0.80 (dead zone)
Authority lowest in CRITICAL (G > 2.0)
```

---

## PART 6: COOLDOWN AND INTERVENTION WINDOWS

### Optimal Intervention Scheduling

**Timing Problem**: When should player activate rituals to maximize suppression effect?

**Answer**: Just before activation threshold, OR repeatedly during dead zone.

**Scenario A: Preemptive Window**

```
G approaching 1.50 threshold (rising activity)
Window: 1.30 < G < 1.50

Player choice:
  Option 1: Do nothing → G crosses 1.50 → STORM activates → hard to undo
  Option 2: Use ritual when G = 1.40 → G → 0.80+ → system stays in dead zone
           → Another ritual after cooldown → G drops to deactivation

Optimal timing: Intervene JUST BEFORE threshold, not after
```

**Scenario B: Recovery Window**

```
G = 1.20 (dead zone, STORM active)
Ritual cooldown just expired

Player activates ritual:
  dG/dt = -0.10 + inflow ≈ -0.07 (net suppression)
  
Time to cross deactivation threshold:
  1.20 → 0.80 takes 1.20 / 0.07 ≈ 17 seconds

At 60-second cooldown:
  Can fit ~3 rituals in recovery window before inflow re-escalates
```

### Intervention Denial (Enemy/NPC Perspective)

**Implication for PvP/PvE**:

```
If players cooperate: Synchronized rituals = effective
If players conflict: 
  - Hostile players can trigger high-inflow scenarios (combat/mining)
  - Suppress rituals become ineffective (inflow >> suppression)
  - Creates "escalation trap": more players/mobs = harder to control

Design consequence: Multiplayer environments naturally escalate
```

---

## PART 7: ADAPTIVE REGULATION & SELF-HEALING

### Stabilization via Behavior Change (Not Rituals)

Player strategy (no rituals required):

```
Phase 1 (Escalation recognition):
  G reaches 1.40 (approaching storm)
  Player slows mining/combat activity
  
Phase 2 (Stabilization):
  M, C drop to baseline (no new inflows)
  G decays: 1.40 → 1.30 → 1.20 ... (slow)
  
Phase 3 (Recovery):
  After 20–30 minutes: G → 0
  System resets to CALM
```

**No ritual resources required, but high time cost.**

### System Self-Regulation (Ecology Feedback)

Suppose no player intervention occurs:

```
G = 1.80 (STORM active)
Spawn multiplier = 2.2× → Mobs dense
Player massacres mobs (forced, not optional)
Combat inflow C surges

But: If player loses battles:
  Stress accumulates locally
  Eventually forces player to adapt behavior
  Activity naturally drops

System regulates through gameplay difficulty!
```

---

## PART 8: PLAYER FEEDBACK SYSTEMS

### Perceptual Feedback (From Perceptual Synthesis Work)

The HUD provides real-time signal of control effectiveness:

```
G rising (red bar growing):       → "Losing battle, need more suppression"
G stable (static red bar):        → "Holding line, maintained balance"
G dropping (bar shrinking):       → "Winning, system calming"
GLOBAL_STORM activated (red UI): → "CRITICAL - Need strong intervention"
GLOBAL_STORM deactivated (green): → "Recovery success"
```

**Effect**: Player adjusts strategy based on visual feedback (no explicit quest log).

### Audio Coupling

```
Spatialized tension stingers:
  - Indicate location of high stress
  - Guide player toward intervention targets
  - Provide urgency signal when approaching thresholds
```

### Ecological Signals

```
Spawn behavior changes:
  CALM:      Passive mobs common, rare hostiles
  STRAINED:  More hostiles, less passives
  CRITICAL:  Aggressive entities, almost no passives
  
Player reads ecosystem state directly from mob mix observed.
```

---

## PART 9: CONTROL AUTHORITY LIMITS

### Fundamental Uncontrollability (Theorem)

**Claim**: There exist initial conditions and inflow rates from which player cannot prevent escalation to STORM.

**Proof**:

```
Suppression rate per ritual: S_ritual ≈ 5 units (single use)
Accumulation rate during sustained activity: I_activity ≈ 0.03 units/sec

If activity is very high (many mobs, heavy mining):
  I_activity ≈ 0.05 units/sec > S_ritual / 60 sec ≈ 0.083 units/sec

Wait, that contradicts. Let me recalculate:

Suppression per ritual: 5 units over 60 ticks = 0.083 units/tick
Inflow: 0.03 units/tick

Suppression > inflow → player CAN suppress.

BUT: What if inflow >> suppression?

High-multiplayer scenario:
  50 players mining/fighting simultaneously
  I_activity ≈ 0.5 units/tick
  Single ritual: 0.083 units/tick

Suppression/inflow ratio: 0.083 / 0.5 = 16.6%

Player can suppress but not reverse growth.
System inevitably escalates to STORM if activity is high enough.

Q.E.D. (Equilibrium theorem, not strict uncontrollability)
```

**Implication**: 
- **Single player can always suppress tension** (achievable ratio ≥ 1)
- **Multiple players cannot always suppress total tension** (ratio < 1 if many active)
- Creates **scaling problem**: large player counts naturally reach STORM

### Design Consequence

This is intentional: the engine is tuned such that:

```
Solo player: Full control (can always avoid STORM)
Small group (2–5): Negotiated control (choices matter)
Large group (20+): Environmental autonomy (storm often active)

Emergent game dynamic: Population density affects controllability
```

---

## PART 10: STRATEGIC CONTROL FRAMEWORK

### Control Theory Classification

**Standard control terminology**:

```
System (autonomous dynamics):
  dG/dt = -λ_global × G + κ × regional_inflow + inflow_from_activity

Input (player intervention):
  u(t) = ritual activation (magnitude and timing)
  
Control objective:
  Keep G < G_threshold (1.50 for CALM, 0.80 for deactivation)
  Minimize ritual resource usage (cooldown-limited)
```

**Reachability**: 
- Can player reach any desired G ∈ [0, 2.5]? **Yes** (via ritual combination)

**Controllability**:
- Can player steer G arbitrarily over time? **Partially**
- Constraints: cooldown limits, saturation reduces effectiveness, activity inflow
- Conclusion: **Constrained controllability**

**Stabilizability**:
- Can player stabilize G at chosen setpoint? **Yes**
- Method: Balance ritual suppression with activity inflow
- Equilibrium: G_eq = inflow / suppression_rate

### Optimal Control Problem

**Statement**: Find intervention schedule u(t) to minimize cost:

```
J = ∫ (G(t) - G_target)² + λ × (ritual_usage)² dt

subject to:
  dG/dt = -λ×G + inflow + u(t)
  0 ≤ u(t) ≤ u_max
  cooldown constraints on u
```

**Solution Sketch** (qualitative):
1. If G > G_target: Activate strongest available ritual (u_max)
2. If G ≈ G_target: Reduce u gradually (avoid oscillation)
3. If G < G_target: Do not intervene (let inflow rebuild)

**Result**: Bang-bang controller (maximum or minimum) with rare intermediate values.

---

## CONCLUSION

The UTD engine provides **meaningful but constrained player control**:

- **Achievable**: Players can suppress tension, prevent escalation, reverse STORM states
- **Costly**: Each intervention uses limited resources (cooldown, saturation)
- **Skill-based**: Optimal timing and sequencing matter
- **Emergent gameplay**: Population density, activity patterns create control challenges

The design ensures **agency without trivialization**: players matter, but the world resists total domination.

---

**See also**: DYNAMICS_AND_STABILITY.md for equilibrium analysis, SYSTEM_OVERVIEW.md for intervention mechanics overview.

## A. CORE STABLE SYSTEMS (do not rewrite)

Architecture-complete, wired, and forming the verified simulation spine. Changes should be **calibration-only** unless fixing bugs.

| File | What it does | Why stable |
|------|----------------|------------|
| `tension/ChunkTensionData.java` | Per-chunk tension map, state machine, contamination, local storm flags, NBT persistence | Single source of local truth; hysteresis and prune logic are integrated |
| `tension/ChunkTensionManager.java` | Facade for mining/combat/local add/reduce | All player sinks go through here |
| `core/GlobalFieldCoupling.java` | Distributed chunk→global inflow; idle-stable when no stressed chunks | Replaces hotspot coupling; `STRESS_BASELINE` gate is structural |
| `core/CouplingSplit.java` | Decomposition DTO for diagnostics | Pure data; no behavior |
| `core/TensionManager.java` | Global scalar dynamics, dual regime, storm hysteresis, corruption tiers | Central global ODE; equilibrium guard |
| `core/TensionServerTick.java` | Server tick orchestration, chunk PDE, sync cadence, global storm FX | Main loop; local storm threshold lives here |
| `core/TensionActivityLedger.java` | Per-tick movement/mining/combat/ritual sums for GLOBAL_FLOW | Attribution only |
| `core/TensionTraceClock.java` | Server tick counter for logs | Trivial, widely referenced |
| `event/TensionEvents.java` | Fabric hooks: break, death, sleep, portal, bulk build, deep layer | Primary player→local ingress |
| `tick/TensionLogger.java` | Bridges actions → trace + DiagnosticLogs + CSV | Standard observability path |
| `core/TensionTraceLogger.java` | `[TRACE]` + `[TENSION_TRACE]` structured stdout | Human tuning trail |
| `diag/DiagnosticLogs.java` | Tagged stdout blocks + SessionTelemetry hooks | Canonical log format |
| `core/RegionDiagnosticsManager.java` | 8×8 region aggregates, hostile bias estimate | Storm drift + sync packet + ecology |
| `network/TensionSyncPacket.java` | Server→client tension snapshot | Client isolation contract |
| `client/TensionSyncState.java` | Client mirror + smoothing + perceivedTension() | Required for feel layer |
| `telemetry/SessionTelemetryExporter.java` | Append-only session CSV + summary | New; non-gameplay |

**Supporting stable (smaller but wired):**

- `signals/PlayerStateIntegrator.java` — long-session fatigue leak
- `event/TensionMobHooks.java` — hostile attribute buffs in stressed chunks
- `ecology/TensionSpawnEcology.java` + `mixin/SpawnHelperMixin.java` — spawn weighting
- `core/TensionEvent.java` — log record type

# DEPENDENCY GRAPH & INFORMATION FLOW
## Complete Map of Tension System Architecture

**Purpose**: Visualize how state variables propagate through the system  
**Format**: Dataflow diagrams, dependency tables, and timing analysis  
**Scope**: All 7 layers from players to UI

---

## 1. SYSTEM LAYERS

The tension system is organized in **7 functional layers**:

```
Layer 0: INPUT       → Player Actions (movement, mining, combat)
Layer 1: INJECTION   → Activity Ledgers & Managers
Layer 2: LOCAL       → Per-Chunk Tension Field & State Machines
Layer 3: AGGREGATION → Regional Summarization
Layer 4: GLOBAL      → World-Level Tension & Storms
Layer 5: OUTPUT      → Diagnostics & Logging
Layer 6: RENDERING   → Client-side HUD, Audio, Visuals
```

---

## 2. DETAILED DATAFLOW

### 2.1 Layer 0: Player Input

**Sources**:
- `PlayerEntity.vel` → Movement detection
- `BlockBreakEvent` → Mining detection  
- `LivingEntity.death` → Combat detection

**Data Points**:
- Player position (chunk coordinates)
- Movement type (walk, sprint, swim, elytra)
- Block ID (mined block)
- Entity type (killed entity)
- Current dimension (Overworld vs. Nether)

**Frequency**: Per-tick + event-driven

---

### 2.2 Layer 1: Injection & Ledgers

**Component 1A**: ChunkTensionManager (API)
- **Input**: Player activity from Layer 0
- **Output**: Injected tension values
- **Functions**:
  - `addLocalTension(world, chunk, amount)` → Direct injection to chunk
  - `addMiningTension(world, chunk, blockId)` → Block-type-scaled injection
  - `addSlaughterTension(world, chunk, entityType)` → Entity-type-scaled injection

**Component 1B**: TensionActivityLedger
- **Input**: All player activities
- **Output**: Accumulated totals for logging
- **State Variables**:
  - `movement` (total cumulative)
  - `mining` (total cumulative)
  - `combat` (total cumulative)
- **Purpose**: Diagnostics & trend analysis (not direct tension dynamics)

**Component 1C**: Dimension Multiplier
- **Rule**: Apply ×1.25 to movement if player in Nether
- **Implementation**: In ChunkTensionManager.addLocalTension()

**Data After Layer 1**:
- Per-chunk injection amount ΔL (tension/tick)
- Accumulated activity metrics

---

### 2.3 Layer 2: Local Field & State Machines

**Component 2A**: ChunkTensionData (Storage)
- **Input**: Injection from Layer 1
- **State Variables**:
  - `localTensions: Map<ChunkPos, Double>` ← $L_{i,j}(t)$
  - `chunkStates: Map<ChunkPos, ChunkState>` ← $\sigma_{i,j}(t)$
  - `stormActive: Map<ChunkPos, Boolean>` ← $s_{i,j}(t)$
  - `recentMiningRate: Map<ChunkPos, Double>` ← $m_{i,j}(t)$
  - `contaminationPeak, contaminationLevel` ← Memory maps
  - `lastAccessMs: Map<ChunkPos, Long>` ← TTL tracking
- **Persistence**: NBT serialization to world file
- **API**: getLocalTension, setLocalTension, getChunkState, etc.

**Component 2B**: TensionServerTick (PDE Solver)
- **Frequency**: Every 5 ticks (250 ms per cycle)
- **Input from Layer 2**:
  - $L_{i,j}^{(n)}$ from ChunkTensionData
  - $g^{(n)}$ from global field
  - Neighbor chunk tensions
- **Computation**:
  - Diffusion term: $D \bar{N}_{i,j}$
  - Nonlinear term: $\Alpha_L(s) L^2$
  - Saturation: $-\Beta L^3$
  - Feedback: $\Gamma g$
  - Decay: $-\Lambda_L L M_{\text{decay}}$
  - Storm drain (if $s_{i,j}$)
  - **Result**: $L_{i,j}^{(n+1)}$
- **Output**: Updated $L_{i,j}$ written back to ChunkTensionData
- **Side Effects**:
  - Computes coupling inflow to global field
  - Detects state transitions
  - Updates contamination tier

**Component 2C**: Chunk State Machine
- **Input**: Updated $L_{i,j}$ from TensionServerTick
- **Logic**: Hysteretic transition (see PHASE_1_SYSTEM_DISCOVERY.md Section 4)
- **Output**: $\sigma_{i,j} \in \{\text{STABLE, STRAINED, FRACTURED, DECOUPLED}\}$

**Component 2D**: Contamination Decay Modulation
- **Input**: Current $L_{i,j}$
- **Rule**: Set $M_{\text{decay}}$ based on thresholds (1.35, 1.15)
- **Memory**: Peak recorded in `contaminationPeak`
- **Effect**: Modulates future decay rates (slower recovery after high tension)

**Data After Layer 2**:
- Updated $L_{i,j}$ field
- New state machine values $\sigma_{i,j}$
- Storm flags $s_{i,j}$
- Coupling inflow metric $I_{\text{inflow}}$

---

### 2.4 Layer 3: Regional Aggregation

**Component**: RegionDiagnosticsManager
- **Frequency**: Every 200 ticks (10 seconds)
- **Spatial Scale**: 8×8 chunk regions (128×128 blocks)
- **Input from Layer 2**:
  - All $L_{i,j}$ from ChunkTensionData
  - All $\sigma_{i,j}$ states
  - Storm flags
- **Computation** (per region):
  - $R_{\text{avg}} = \text{mean}(L_{i,j})$ over region
  - $R_{\text{max}} = \max(L_{i,j})$ over region
  - $R_{\text{strained}} = \#\{\sigma_{i,j} = \text{STRAINED}\}$
  - $R_{\text{fractured}} = \#\{\sigma_{i,j} \in \{\text{FRACTURED, DECOUPLED}\}\}$
  - $R_{\text{storm}} = \text{any}(s_{i,j})$
  - $H_{\text{bias}} = 0.32(R_{\text{avg}} - 0.55) + 0.12 R_{\text{max}} + 0.14 \mathbb{1}[R_{\text{storm}}]$
- **Ecological Classification**:
  - CALM: $R_{\text{max}} < 1.35$ AND $R_{\text{avg}} \leq 1.0$
  - STRAINED: $R_{\text{avg}} > 1.0$ AND $R_{\text{max}} < 1.35$
  - FRACTURED: $R_{\text{max}} \geq 1.35$
- **Cache**: CACHE map updated every 200 ticks
- **Output**: RegionSnapshot objects for logging + hostile spawn bias calculation

**Data After Layer 3**:
- Regional aggregates (8×8 chunk summaries)
- Ecological state classifications
- Spawn bias metrics

---

### 2.5 Layer 4: Global Field Dynamics

**Component 4A**: TensionServerTick (Coupling Computation)
- **Frequency**: Every tick (50 ms)
- **Input from Layer 2**:
  - Mean $\mu_L = \text{mean}(L_{i,j})$
  - Stdev $\sigma_L = \text{stdev}(L_{i,j})$
  - Storm fraction $f_{\text{storm}}$
  - Fractured fraction $f_{\text{frac}}$
- **Computation**:
  - $I_{\text{ambient}} = 0.0035 \mu_L$
  - $I_{\text{hotspot}} = 0.052 \sigma_L + 0.14 f_{\text{storm}} + 0.26 f_{\text{frac}}$
  - $I_{\text{inflow}} = I_{\text{ambient}} + I_{\text{hotspot}}$
- **Output**: $I_{\text{inflow}}$ passed to TensionManager

**Component 4B**: TensionManager (Global PDE)
- **Frequency**: Every tick (50 ms)
- **Input**:
  - Previous global tension $g^{(n)}$
  - Coupling inflow $I_{\text{inflow}}$ from Component 4A
  - Ritual impulse buffer $I_{\text{ritual}}$
- **Computation**:
  - Decay: $\Delta g_{\text{decay}} = -\Lambda_g g$
  - Nonlinearity: $\Delta g_{\text{nonlin}} = \Alpha_g g^2$
  - Total update: $\Delta g = \Delta g_{\text{decay}} + \Delta g_{\text{nonlin}} + I_{\text{inflow}} + I_{\text{ritual}}$
  - Clamp: $g^{(n+1)} = \text{clamp}(g + \Delta g, 0, 5.0)$
- **Side Effect**: Update corruption tier based on $g$
- **Output**: Updated $g^{(n+1)}$

**Component 4C**: Global Storm State Machine
- **Input**: Updated $g^{(n+1)}$
- **Logic**: 
  - Onset if $g > 1.0$
  - Offset if $g < 0.7$
- **Output**: $s_{\text{global}} \in \{\text{true, false}\}$

**Data After Layer 4**:
- Updated global field $g(t)$
- Global storm flag
- Corruption tier
- Feedback term $\Gamma g$ (sent back to Layer 2)

---

### 2.6 Layer 5: Diagnostics & Logging

**Component 5A**: TensionTraceLogger
- **Input**: System events (state transitions, storms, equilibrium damps, etc.)
- **Frequency**: Event-driven + periodic (every 20 ticks)
- **Output**: Trace log file with timestamps

**Component 5B**: DiagnosticLogs
- **Input**: 
  - Per-20-tick global flow summary
  - Regional snapshots (every 200 ticks)
  - Storm start/end events
- **Output**: Diagnostic CSV/JSON files for analysis

**Component 5C**: TensionActivityLedger (Activity Tracking)
- **Input**: Accumulated movement, mining, combat metrics
- **Output**: Included in global flow logs

**Data After Layer 5**:
- Log files with complete trace history
- Performance metrics
- Event journals

---

### 2.7 Layer 6: Client-Side Rendering & Audio

**Component 6A**: Packet Network
- **Input**: TensionSyncPacket (every 20 ticks from server)
  - Global tension $g$
  - Global storm active flag
  - Corruption tier
- **Frequency**: Every 20 ticks (1 second) sync interval
- **Protocol**: Server → Client unicast to all players

**Component 6B**: Client-Side HUD (TensionHud.java + enhancements)
- **Input**: Synced global/local tension from packet
- **Rendering**:
  - Color progression: 🟢 (STABLE) → 🟡 (STRAINED) → 🟠 (FRACTURED) → 🔴 (DECOUPLED)
  - Numerical tension display
  - Storm indicator
  - Corruption level text
- **Frequency**: Every frame (client-side)

**Component 6C**: Audio Coupling (TensionAudioCoupling.java)
- **Input**: Local chunk tension + global field
- **Audio Effects**:
  - Ambient sound intensity scales with local tension
  - Pitch/frequency modulation
  - Spatialization (tension gradient affects audio direction)
- **Frequency**: Per-tick client-side

**Component 6D**: Debug Overlay (TensionDebugOverlay.java)
- **Input**: Raw tension metrics (toggle F8)
- **Display**: Detailed per-chunk stats, coupling terms, state info
- **Frequency**: Every frame (when enabled)

**Component 6E**: StormManager (Client Storm Effects)
- **Input**: 
  - Global + regional tension
  - Region coordinates
  - Hot region tracking
- **Visual Effects**:
  - Lightning strikes (intensity ∝ tension)
  - Weather intensity
  - Thunder sounds
- **Frequency**: Per-tick throttled

**Data After Layer 6**:
- Visual HUD on screen
- Audio cues to player
- Particle effects
- Console/debug output

---

---

## 4. FEEDBACK LOOPS

### 4.1 Local-to-Global Loop

```
L_{i,j} (high) 
    ↓
f_storm ↑, f_frac ↑
    ↓
I_inflow ↑
    ↓
g ↑ (global)
    ↓
Γg term ↑ in PDE
    ↓
L_{i,j} increases further  [POSITIVE FEEDBACK]
```

**Effect**: Creates regions of mutual amplification where chunk + global tension reinforce.

### 4.2 Nonlinear Threshold Loop

```
L_{i,j} → 0.95 (crosses T_1)
    ↓
s_{i,j} = true (local storm)
    ↓
ALPHA_L × 1.8
    ↓
L_{i,j} growth accelerates
    ↓
Maintained until L < 0.25  [HYSTERETIC SWITCH]
```

**Effect**: Once a chunk storms, it maintains storm state even if global field drops, until local decay brings L below 0.25.

### 4.3 Contamination Memory Loop

```
L_{i,j} > 1.35
    ↓
M_decay = 0.38 (62% suppression)
    ↓
Recovery rate = LAMBDA_L × 0.38
    ↓
Slow recovery, tension persists
    ↓
If L drops < 1.15 → M_decay = 1.0  [GRADUAL RELEASE]
```

**Effect**: High-tension events leave a "scar" that slows subsequent recovery.

### 4.4 Global-Local-Regional Loop

```
Many chunks strained (σ_ij = STRAINED)
    ↓
f_frac ↑
    ↓
I_hotspot ↑ (0.26 × f_frac)
    ↓
g ↑
    ↓
Γg term affects all chunks
    ↓
Tendency toward global escalation
    ↓
Breaks only when regional relaxation > inflow  [EQUILIBRIUM COMPETITION]
```

---

## 5. TIMING & SYNCHRONIZATION

### 5.1 Multi-Rate Update Schedule

```
Tick 0 (50 ms)    │ Movement inject
                  │ TensionServerTick (coupling calc)
                  │ TensionManager (global PDE)
                  │ ...
                  │
Tick 1 (50 ms)    │ Mining inject
                  │ TensionServerTick (coupling calc)
                  │ TensionManager (global PDE)
                  │ ...
                  │
Tick 2 (50 ms)    │ ...
├─ Tick 4 (50 ms) │ ...
└─ Tick 5 (250 ms)│ CHUNK PDE UPDATE (L_ij = L_ij + ΔL)
                  │ State machine update
                  │ Contamination tier update
                  │
Tick 20 (1 s)     │ NETWORK SYNC
                  │ Client packets
                  │ Diagnostics log
                  │
Tick 200 (10 s)   │ REGIONAL AGGREGATION
                  │ Cache refresh (RegionDiagnosticsManager)
                  │ Regional diagnostics log
```

### 5.2 State Variable Update Frequencies

| State Variable | Update Frequency | Period | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| $L_{i,j}$ (local tension) | Every 5 ticks | 250 ms | TensionServerTick PDE |
| $s_{i,j}$ (local storm) | Every 5 ticks | 250 ms | State machine |
| $\sigma_{i,j}$ (chunk state) | Every 5 ticks | 250 ms | State machine |
| $M_{\text{decay}, i,j}$ | Every 5 ticks | 250 ms | Contamination logic |
| Activity injection | Every tick | 50 ms | Player actions |
| $I_{\text{inflow}}$ (coupling) | Every tick | 50 ms | TensionServerTick coupling |
| $g$ (global tension) | Every tick | 50 ms | TensionManager |
| $s_{\text{global}}$ (global storm) | Every tick | 50 ms | Global state machine |
| $c$ (corruption tier) | Every tick | 50 ms | TensionManager |
| $R_{\text{avg}}, R_{\text{max}}, ...$ (regional) | Every 200 ticks | 10 s | RegionDiagnosticsManager |
| Client HUD | Every frame | ~16–50 ms | Renderer |
| Audio | Every frame | ~16–50 ms | Audio engine |
| Trace log | Per-event | Variable | TensionTraceLogger |
| Diagnostic log | Every 20 ticks | 1 s | DiagnosticLogs |

---

## 6. PERSISTENCE & RECOVERY

### 6.1 Serialization Points

**On World Save**:
1. ChunkTensionData → World NBT compound
   - `localTensions` map
   - `chunkStates` map
   - `recentMiningRate` map
   - `stormActive` map
   - `contaminationPeak, contaminationLevel`

**On World Load**:
1. NBT → ChunkTensionData (via fromNbt)
2. Game ticks begin, coupling dynamics restart

### 6.2 Recovery Guarantees

- ✅ Local tension field fully restored
- ✅ State machines restored to previous state
- ✅ Storm flags preserved
- ✅ Contamination memory preserved (slower recovery)
- ✅ Global tension: **NOT PERSISTED** (resets to 0.0 on load)

**Note**: Global tension is recomputed from local field statistics via coupling, so world reload can cause transient stability changes.

---
